## GROUP PROFILE

Gallant Venture Ltd. (' GV '), headquartered and listed in Singapore, is an Indonesia focused investment holding company with operations primarily in the islands of Batam and Bintan, Indonesia. Our business is well-positioned to leverage on the strategic alliance between Singapore and Indonesia governments and close proximity to Singapore. The principal business of GV and its subsidiaries (' the Group ') are utilities provider, master planner and developer of industrial parks, property development  and  resort  operations.  Through  the  Group's  associated  companies'  PT  Indomobil  Sukses  International Tbk (' PT IMAS ') and BOMC Pte Ltd (' BOMC '), the Group has foothold in the automotive and offshore marine industry.

<!-- image -->

## GROUP BUSINESSES

## INDUSTRIAL PARKS

We  own  and  manage  Batamindo  Industrial  Park  (' BIP ')  and  Bintan Industrial Estate (' BIE ') in Batam and Bintan that offer the convenience of one-stop manufactory environment with ready access to Singapore's financial,  infrastructure  and  logistics  network.  BIP,  a  320  ha  premium industrial park in Batam with net factory lettable area of approximately 577,000  sqm,  consists  of  200  ready-built-factories  with  factory  size between 972 sqm and 9,217 sqm and other services include logistics, medical amenities and workers' accommodation.

BIP is the first industrial park in Asia-Pacific certified ISO 9001 and ISO 14001. BIE, a 170 ha industrial park in Bintan with net factory lettable area of approximately 107,000 sqm, consists of 88 ready-built-factories with factory size between 504 sqm and 2,100 sqm and has its own dedicated wharf, CIQP facilities and amenities for medical and housing.

## UTILITIES

<!-- image -->

## PROPERTY DEVELOPMENT

We  are  the  master  planner  and  developer  for  industrial,  commercial, residential  and  resorts  development  in  Bintan  and  Batam,  Indonesia. We own approximately 18,000 ha of land in Bintan. Our integrated resort development, Lagoi Bay, consists of 1,300 ha earmarked for development of resorts, residences, shopping, restaurants, entertainments and seasport facilities.

Some of the investors in Lagoi Bay include world's leading resorts and hotels  marques such as The Sanchaya, Four Point by Sheraton, Hotel Indigo and Hotel Inn by InterContinental Hotels Group, Alila Villas Bintan, Dialoog Hotel by Malka and The Haven Bintan Lagoi Bay by the Haven Group.

<!-- image -->

We are independent utilities provider to our industrial park and resorts in Batam and Bintan. We provide power, water, waste management services and  telecommunication  services  to  our  customers  in  the  industrial parks and resorts. We have invested approximately S$435 million in the construction  and  development  of  utilities  infrastructure  and  resources that include power generation and distribution facilities, portable water treatment  facilities,  sewage  treatment  plants,  waste  water  treatment facilities and landfills.

For power generation, we have aggregate installed capacity of 154 MW, of which 120 MW in BIP, 10 MW in BIIE and 24 MW in Resorts. In order to  cope  with  any  potential  increase  in  power  consumption,  we  cater approximately 30% of our installed capacity as standby reserve. For water and waste treatment, we have aggregate capacity of 14,500m 3 /day and 10,000m 3 /day. For telecommunication services, we have approximately 6,000  lines  servicing  Batam  and  Bintan  and  also  provide  internet  and triple play (Telephone, Internet and IPTv) services.

<!-- image -->

## RESORT OPERATIONS

We  are  the  master  planner  and  developer  in  Bintan  Resorts.  We undertake the overall planning, development, operations and marketing of  Bintan Resorts. We provide support facilities and services including ferry  services  and  ferry  terminal  operations,  security,  tour  and  resort operations, property rental, workers' accommodation, medical support and estate and township maintenance.

## OTHER BUSINESS

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

PT IMAS, an associated company of the Group, is an Indonesia based integrated automotive group. Its main business among others dealership franchise  holder,  vehicle  sales  distributor,  after  sales  services,  vehicle financing, spare parts distributor, vehicle assembler, automotive parts/ component manufacturer, car rental services, used cars trading, logistics services,  fuel  distribution,  non-formal  education  services,  and  other related  supporting  services.  PT  IMAS  holds  well-known  brands  of international reputation, namely Audi, Nissan, Volkswagen, Suzuki, KIA, Jaguar, Land Rover, Hino, Renault Trucks, Volvo, John Deere, Kalmar, Bandit, Datsun, HIAB, SDLG and Manitou.

BOMC,  an  associated  company  of  the  Group,  own  and  operate a  state-of-the  art  common-user  facility  in  Bintan  Industrial  Estate  for manufacturing,  fabrication,  repairs,  modifications,  and  logistics  for  the offshore  marine,  oil  and  gas  industries.  It  offers  all  offshore  projects the  distinct  advantage  to  lower  costs,  provides  increased  efficiencies and productivity, adhere to strict corporate compliance and governance requirements with all operations conducted under world class safety and quality systems.

## BOARD OF DIRECTORS

## MR LIM HOCK SAN

Non-Executive Chairman and Independent Director

Date of last election: 29 April 2022

Board Committee:

Chairman, Audit and Risk Management Committee

Chairman, Nominating Committee

Member, Remuneration Committee

Mr  Lim  was  appointed  as  a  Non-Executive  Chairman  and Independent Director on 1 February 2006.

Mr Lim has a Bachelor of Accountancy from the University of  Singapore  and  a  Master  of  Science  (Management) from  Massachusetts  Institute  of  Technology.  Mr  Lim  also attended the Advanced Management Programme at Harvard Business School. He is a Fellow of The Chartered Institute of  Management  Accountants  (UK)  and  a  Fellow  and  past President of the Institute of Singapore Chartered Accountants. He is also a recipient of the Singapore Government Meritorious Service Medal, the Public Administration Medal (Gold) and the Public Service Medal.

Current directorship in other companies listed on Singapore stock exchange - Indofood Agri Resources Ltd

| ## MR EUGENE CHO PARK  Executive Director and Chief Executive Officer  Date of last election: 30 April 2021 Board Committee:  Nil   |
|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

Mr Park was appointed as an Executive Director and Chief Executive Director on 1 February 2006.

Responsible  for  the  overall  management  of  the  Company, Mr  Park  is  a  co-founder  of  Parallax  Capital  Management Group.  He  has  also  spent  more  than  15  years  as  an investment banker with Credit Suisse First Boston in London, Chase Manhattan Asia Ltd in Hong Kong and Banque Paribas in Singapore. He received a Bachelor of Arts (Chemistry) from Princeton University  in  the  United  States  of  America  and  a Master of Business Administration from INSEAD in France.

## MR GIANTO GUNARA

Executive Director and Chief Operating Officer

Date of last election: 26 June 2020

Board Committee:

Nil

Mr  Gunara  was  appointed  as  an  Executive  Director  on 8 November 2006.

Mr Gunara is the Executive Director/Group Chief Operating Officer  overseeing  the  Group  Operations.  He  started  his career  with  Haagtechno  BV-Den  Bosch  in  Holland  as  a Management  Trainee  in  1984  and  is  currently  sits  on  the Board of Director/Commissioner of several subsidiaries of the Group and in other private enterprises.

He holds a Bachelor in Business Administration degree from Simon Fraser University, Vancouver, Canada.

## MR CHOO KOK KIONG

Executive Director and Chief Financial Officer

Date of last election: 29 April 2022 Board Committee: Nil

Mr Choo was appointed as an Executive Director on 30 April 2014.

Mr  Choo  is  the  Executive  Director/Group  Chief  Financial Officer overseeing the Group and its corporate services. He is  also  appointed  as  a  Group  Risk  Officer.  Prior  to  joining the company, he held various management positions in the Sembcorp group. He has over 30 years of finance experience, having  held  the  positions  of  Vice-President  of  Finance  at Sembcorp Parks Management and Sembcorp Parks Holdings Ltd (now known as Sembcorp Development Ltd), Assistant Vice-President  of  Finance  at  Sembcorp  Industries  Ltd  and Accounts Manager with Singapore Precision Industries Pte Ltd.  Mr  Choo  also  sits  on  the  board  of  QAF  Limited  and Singapore  Kitchen  Equipment  Limited  as  non-executive director and non-executive independent director, respectively.

He  holds  a  Master  in  Business  Administration  from  the University  of  Wales  (UK)/Manchester  Business  School (UK).  He  had  also  qualifications  from  Chartered  Institute  of Management  Accountants  (CIMA,  UK)  and  Association  of Chartered Certified Accountants (ACCA, UK).

Current  directorship  in  other  company  listed  on  Singapore stock exchange

- QAF Limited

- Singapore Kitchen Equipment Limited

## DR TAN CHIN NAM

Non-Executive Director

Date of last election: 30 April 2021 Board Committee: Nil

Dr Tan was appointed as a Non-Executive Director on 25 May 2009.

Dr  Tan  is  currently  Chairman  of  Global  Fusion  Capital  Pte Ltd.  He  is  also  Senior  Adviser  to  the  Salim  Group  as  well as  Member  of  the  Centre  for  Liveable  Cities  Distinguished Advisers Panel, the Board of Trustees of Bankinter Foundation for  Innovation  (Spain),  Eight  Inc  Advisory  Board  and  the Singapore Symphony Orchestra Council.

Dr Tan had 34 years of distinguished service in the Singapore Public  Service  holding  various  key  appointments  before completing his term as a Permanent Secretary in 2007. He held  various  top  public  leadership  positions  including  as General Manager and Chairman, National Computer Board; Managing  Director,  Economic  Development  Board;  Chief Executive, Singapore Tourism Board; Permanent Secretary, Ministry  of  Manpower;  Permanent  Secretary,  Ministry  of Information,  Communications  and  the  Arts;  Chairman, National Library Board; and Chairman, Media Development Authority.  He  played  a  leading  role  in  the  information technology,  economic,  tourism,  manpower,  library,  media, arts and creative industries development of Singapore. Dr Tan subsequently held directorship and advisory roles at various public and private companies.

Dr Tan holds degrees in industrial engineering and economics from the University of Newcastle, Australia and an MBA from the University of Bradford, UK. He also holds two honorary doctorate degrees awarded by both universities. He attended an Advanced Management Programme at Harvard Business School  and  is  an  Eisenhower  Fellow.  He  was  awarded four  Public  Administration  Medals  by  the  Government  of Singapore.

## MR AXTON SALIM

Non-Executive Director

Date of last election: 29 April 2022 Board Committee:

Nil

Mr  Axton  was  appointed  as  a  Non-Executive  Director  on 30 April 2014.

Mr Axton was first appointed as a Director of PT Indofood Sukses Makmur Tbk based on the resolution of the AGM in 2009 with the latest re-elected in 2021. He heads the Dairy Division and is concurrently the Director of PT Indofood CBP Sukses  Makmur  Tbk,  Non-Executive  Director  of  Indofood Agri  Resources Ltd and First Pacific Company Ltd, as well as Commissioner of PT Salim Ivomas Pratama Tbk and PT London  Sumatra  Indonesia  Tbk.  He  started  his  career  in PT  Indofood  Fritolay  Makmur  as  a  Brand  Manager  before appointed  as  an  Assistant  CEO  of  PT  Indofood  Sukses Makmur Tbk.

Mr  Axton  is  also  part  of  the  United  Nations  Scaling  Up Nutrition (SUN) Movement Business Network Advisory Group, where he serves as a Co-Chair, and SUN Business Network Indonesia  as  a  Coordinator,  as  well  as  Nanyang  Business School as Advisory Board Member.

He holds a Bachelor of Science in Business Administration from University of Colorado, USA.

Current  directorship  in  other  company  listed  on  Singapore stock exchange

- Indofood Agri Resources Ltd

## BOARD OF DIRECTORS

## MR FOO KO HING

Non-Executive and Independent Director

Date of last election: 30 April 2021 Board Committee: Chairman, Remuneration Committee

Member, Audit and Risk Management Committee

Member, Nominating Committee

Mr  Foo  was  appointed  as  an  Independent  Director  on 8 December 2004.

After  leaving  PricewaterhouseCoopers  in  1986,  Mr  Foo joined  The  Hongkong  and  Shanghai  Banking  Corporation Limited ('HSBC') group in the Trust and Fiduciary Business in Singapore. He was later seconded to HSBC Bank Jersey C.I in 1989 and was subsequently promoted to Executive Director, covering fiduciary activities, private banking, compliance and investment functions. He returned to Singapore in 1991 and resumed  responsibilities  with  the  HSBC  Investment  Bank Group for Private Banking and Trust Services as an Executive Director and Head of Business Development. Mr Foo is also a  co-founder  and  Director  of  Cerealtech  Pte  Ltd,  a  food technology company specialising in enzyme application and micro ingredient development for the industrial baking sector. He has over 15 years of experience in investment origination, structuring,  monitoring  and  strategic  growth  assistance, with emphasis on the private equity investment and capital markets. He has previously served on the boards of various companies in various sectors listed on the SGX-ST.

He holds a BA Honours Degree in Economics and Accounting from University of Newcastle Upon Tyne, United Kingdom.

## LETTER TO SHAREHOLDERS

Mr Eugene Cho Park Executive Director and Chief Executive Officer

<!-- image -->

As  we  look  back  on  2022  and  look  forward  to  2023,  we are pleased to report that our company has weathered the pandemic and is well positioned to thrive in the coming years. Indonesia  enters  2023  with  around  70%  of  the  population vaccinated, and a record number of 135.6 million employed persons in the work force. In 2022 the economy grew at over 5.3% on the back of strong demand for commodities such as coal, palm oil and base metals, as well as a 5.1% increase household  consumption  (notably  travel  related  spending -  transportation  and  hospitality).  Manufacturing  GDP  is  at record levels, and these non-commodity exports now make up almost half of the $292 billion total exports, contributing to a record trade surplus of $54 billion. The manufacturing sector also saw an increase in FDI of 57%, partially attributable to the  simplified  procedures  of  the  Online  Single  Submission (' OSS ')  and the relaxation of restricted investment areas in the 2021 Omnibus law. And the November 2022 hosting of the G20 meeting in Bali showcased Indonesia's ability to act as a statesman on the world stage.

Mr Lim Hock San Non-Executive Chairman and Independent Director

These factors have had a direct positive impact on a number of our businesses:

- Strong  commodity  prices  increased  demand  for  heavy equipment sales, leasing and spare parts - all of which showed  strong  growth  in  our  associated  company, PT IMAS's businesses.
- Travel  related  spending  increased  our  Bintan  Resorts businesses both directly from increasing visitor arrivals as well as indirectly from increased utility demand from the hotels and businesses in Bintan Resorts.
- Strong domestic growth increased interest in purchase of passenger vehicles by almost 18% year on year.
- Increased  manufacturing  activity  was  reflected  in  our high occupancy rates in our industrial parks, as well as indirectly in increased utility consumption.
- Increased  FDI  led  to  the  new  factories  currently  being built (handing over in 2023 and 2024) whose results will be reflected in next year numbers.

## LETTER TO SHAREHOLDERS

We are also heartened by the fact that in 2022 the Indonesian government was successful (albeit with some initial negative press  and  complaints)  in  the  increase  in  electricity,  diesel and petrol prices - thereby taking the first step in Indonesia's energy transition and reducing subsidies going forward.

There are however some downside risks in 2023.

- A  potential  weakening  of  the  global  economy  on  the back of inflation and higher interest rates in the USA and Europe  could  reduce  demand  from  our  manufacturing tenants.
- There  is  also  a  risk  of  postponement  of  some  FDI  in the  period  of  uncertainty  that  typically  precedes  the Presidential elections scheduled for February 2024.
- Geo-economic fragmentation, if unchecked, could also cause reallocation of FDI to other locations.
- Inflation could also reduce household consumption and potentially travel expenditures.

However,  we  should  note  that  even  during  the  pandemic, China remained the largest trading partner of Indonesia. We expect to see a significant uptick in growth as the Chinese economy accelerates out of their severe Covid restrictions. Last  year  global  growth  was  good,  but  the  strength  of  the European and US economies was partially offset by China's weakness. In 2023, if high rates slow the European and US economies, global growth may still be reasonable because of the strength of the Chinese economy post reopening. The Chinese economy reopening should also have a significant positive  effect  on  their  Asian  trading  partners  including Indonesia. Similarly, the full re-openings of Japan, India and Korea  will  benefit  Indonesia  as  some  of  its  largest  export partners.

While we are aware of the increasing onshoring/near shoring/ friend shoring arguments, we are hopeful that calmer heads will review the logic of incurring enormous capital investment and  long-term  subsidies  to  shift  production  to  unproven but more convenient locations. We believe using economic policies  to  achieve  political  goals  can  result  in  inefficient allocation  of  capital  and  reduce  global  trade  and  global growth.  That  being  said,  there  is  a  clear  trend  to  de-risk manufacturing  exposure  through  diversifying  geographical exposure.  We  believe  that  our  Bintan/Batam  twinned  with Singapore  location  will  allow  us  to  capture  an  increasing amount  of  this  segment  especially  since  we  effectively collocate our manufacturing with the super-efficient flexible and world class multi-modal Singapore logistics infrastructure.

We believe that the strength of the Indonesian economy, both export and domestic, will continue to grow our businesses in 2023 and beyond. We are also hopeful that the 'revenge travel trend' dominating US and European tourism in 2021-22, will be reflected in the Chinese and Asian traveler market in 2023 and  further  contribute  to  our  businesses  rebound  in  2023. We  believe  that  Indonesia's  non-confrontational  neutrality, twinned with the fair-mindedness of Singapore, puts us in a good  political  position  to  continue  to  attract  non-politically sensitive manufacturing FDI to our industrial parks.

Therefore,  we  believe  that  our  portfolio  of  investments, that  combine  our  Indonesian  and  Singaporean  competitive advantages,  will  continue  to  provide  an  excellent  base  for us to grow and prosper in the years to come. Thank you for continuing to support us on our journey.

Sincerely,

| MR LIM HOCK SAN Non-Executive Chairman Independent Director  MR EUGENE CHO PARK Chief Executive Officer Executive Director   |
|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

## MACROECONOMIC ENVIRONMENT

After nearly three years of disruption caused by COVID-19, global  economies  and  businesses  are  finally  returning  to normalcy. Singapore lifted  all  COVID-19  border  restrictions in 2022 and hosted several international events, such as the F1 night  race,  attracting  tourists  and  businesses.  Similarly, COVID-19-related travel restrictions eased as most countries attempted to revive their economy. Several Southeast Asia countries witnessed a strong recovery in their tourism industry after  reopening.  All  are  hopeful  that  global  tourism  will return  to  pre-Covid  level  as  China  gradually  opens  up  its border restrictions and our Bintan Resorts posed to benefit from  outbound  from  China.  While  the  tourism  industry  is recovering,  businesses  are  experiencing  strong  headwinds from inflation and geopolitical risks.

Geopolitical risks continue to dominate the world scene. The war in Ukraine has caused business disruption and resulted in  elevated  energy  costs,  increased  food  prices,  disrupted supply chains, and severally impacted downstream activities, especially in developing countries. In 2022, the US Federal Reserve increased interest rates seven times to tame inflation which consequentially resulted in higher borrowing costs. The continuing  escalation  of  tensions  between  the  two  largest economies,  the  US  and  China,  could  have  a  significant impact on international trade, potentially changing the global operating  environment  and  disrupting  business  operations and supply chains. With de-escalation unlikely to happen in the short term, these could pose significant risks to the global economy in 2023 and beyond.

Indonesia has returned to a pre-pandemic economic growth path after going through the COVID-19 pandemic. Indonesia's economy grew 5.31% as the government eased COVID-19

## BUSINESS REVIEW

restrictions and businesses slowly returned to normalcy. The growth was supported by elevated household consumption and  rising  commodity  prices,  where  Indonesia  is  a  major exporter of commodities such as coal, palm oil, and nickel, which  contributed  to  a  healthier  balance  of  payments. Indonesia has undertaken several initiatives to sustain robust growth, which include:

- Continue to implement tax reforms to create space for investments and create resilience to external shocks;
- Boost the growth of exports for services; and
- To boost investment in infrastructure and human capital development; and
- Trade reform tap into the growing demand for goods and technologies to reduce carbon emissions, enable the  climate  transition,  and  access  lower-cost  and higher-quality  environmentally  friendly  products  and services.

2022 was a historic year for Indonesia as the nation hosted the G20 summit for the first time in Bali, Indonesia. Indonesia is the only Southeast Asian nation to become a G20 member and the first Southeast Asia country to host this prestigious summit. Indonesia has demonstrated its big role on the world stage  by  pulling  together  a  creditable  series  of  events  and concluding the summit successfully.

The  Rise  of  Indonesia  on  the  global  stage  and  economic powerhouse  in  this  region  will  benefit  the  growth  of  the Group's  core  businesses  in  Indonesia,  where  the  Group operates.

<!-- image -->

## BUSINESS REVIEW

## FINANCIAL HIGHLIGHTS

The Group's revenue increased by 15% to S$166.8 million in FY2022, driven by higher contributions from its industrial parks,  utilities,  and  resort  segments.  With  the  recovery  of global  travel  and  tourism  in  2H2022,  our  resort  segment saw an increase of S$15.2 million in revenue compared to FY2021.

Coupled  with  the  turnaround  of  PT  IMAS  to  profitability  in FY2022, the Group's loss attributable to the owners of the Company has decreased significantly from S$55.6 million to S$13.6 million in FY2022.

At the Group level, the net asset value per share for FY2022 was 13.76 cents compared to 14.90 cents in FY2021. The Group's basic and diluted loss per share was 0.25 cents in FY2022 compared to FY2021's 1.02 cents.

Barring  unforeseen  circumstances,  the  Group  expects revenue  and  contribution  from  its  core  business  segments and  PT  IMAS  to  continue  to  grow  in  2023.  With  a  strong development pipeline in our Batamindo Industrial Park, the Group is on track to achieve profitability soon.

## KEY BUSINESS SEGMENTS

## INDUSTRIAL PARKS

Our  industrial  parks  business  has  continued  to  outperform with a 9.4% y-o-y increase in revenue, driven by the strong demand  for  the  industrial  space.  The  Group  expects  an increase  of  approximately  5.3%  and  16.3%  in  2023  and

<!-- image -->

2024 from the new-build factories. The Group will continue to  invest  and  expand  the  industrial  parks  as  the  demand for  industrial  spaces  is  expected  to  remain  buoyant  due  to tenants' business expansions and the colocation of its supply chain.  The  Group  believes  its  strong  product  offering  and business-friendly  local  authority  will  sustain  growth  in  the near term. The following are updates for our industrial parks in Batam and Bintan, Indonesia:

## (i) Batamindo Industrial Park (' BIP ') (Batam)

In 2022, 7 new factories totaling approximately 18,600 sqm were completed and handed over to the tenants, bringing  BIP  to  full  occupancy.  Currently,  19  new factories, approximately 137,000  sqm,  including one  customised  factory  of  85,000  sqm,  are  under construction, and we expect to hand them over to the tenants over the next 2 years. From 2019, BIP's total factory lettable area has increased by 3.3%, and with the  completion  of  the  19  new  factories,  BIP's  total factory  lettable  area  will  increase  by  another  23.6% by 2024.

With  the  full  occupancy  in  the  industrial  park  and demand remaining high for industrial spaces, the Group has earmarked approximately 40.15 ha of vacant land for expansion.

## (ii) Bintan Industrial Estate (' BIE ') (Bintan)

BIE has performed well this year. During the year, BIE has secured net new leases of 12,068 sqm of factory spaces and a new lease of 13,500 sqm of land. The factory occupancy rate has climbed from 65% to 69%, while  the  average  rental  yield  improved  by  11.8%. The Group has a few new pipelines under discussion with several food-related companies to take up to 10 factories for their expansion in BIE.

The Group has recently signed MOU with one of the leading aviation schools to explore the development of a flying school in Bintan to train airline pilots. The Group will continue to pursue aerospace industries to set up their facilities in BIE, leveraging on its proximity to the future Bintan International Airport.

In  December  2022,  BIE  was  awarded  the  Top  3 best  halal  industrial  estates  in  Indonesia.  It  is  one  of the  Indonesian  government's  strategic  initiatives  to develop the country's halal industry to make Indonesia a  global  halal  producer  hub  in  2024.  This  put  BIE  in an  advantageous  position  to  capture  this  business segment.

## UTILITIES

Utilities  consumption  and  revenue  in  our  industrial  parks segment have increased by 2.0% and 1.9% in tandem with the increase in factory occupancy and land lease. The Group expects an incremental increase of approximately 8.0% and 21.8% in 2023 and 2024 from the new-build factories. With the recovery of travel and tourism in the region, the utilities consumption and revenue in the resort  segment  increased significantly  by  70.2%  and  79.3%,  respectively.  The  Group is  optimistic that the revenue from this segment will further improve in 2023 with the lifting of travel restrictions in China.

We  are  on  track  to  achieving  our  solar  panel  deployment target of at least 12 megawatts (MWp) by early 2023, which could supply up to 20% of green energy to our tenants in BIP. Subsequently, the Group will deploy green energy solutions in BIE and Bintan Resorts. In addition, the Group has allocated up to 1,000 ha of land in Bintan for further development of solar farms to increase the supply of green energy in middle term.  We  are  exploring  emerging  low-carbon  technologies such as green hydrogen and carbon capture that will help to reduce our carbon footprint in the longer term.

We are  gradually  transiting  from  traditional  fuel  sources  to greener fuel sources for power generation. We are investing in clean production technologies to change how we provide electricity to our tenants and customers. The Group believes that by positioning our industrial parks and Bintan Resorts as eco-industrial parks and sustainable tourism destinations, we will be able to sustain our growth.

## PROPERTY DEVELOPMENT

We  remain  optimistic  about  the  long-term  potential  of  our property  development  business,  notwithstanding  that  we ended  the  year  without  any  land  sales.  Over  the  years, our  project  team  has  developed  in-house  capabilities and  expertise  in  project  management  and  infrastructure development.  We  can  cost-effectively  optimize  our  internal resources for our in-house maintenance, restoration, renovation,  development,  and  upgrading  of  our  group's infrastructure and assets in Bintan Resorts. In addition, our project team has been providing construction management support for constructing our new factories in BIP. However, these  activities  may  not  translate  into  revenue  for  the segment; the Group has wholly benefited in cost saving and lower capital investment.

While the Group continues to pursue land sales, we decided not to depend purely on lumpy land sales. As such, we have been  focusing  on  development  that  will  benefit  our  resort segment in Bintan to grow a sustainable and recurring income derived from these developments.

Currently,  the  Group  is  focusing  on  master  planning  and development of several projects in Bintan, which include:

- (i) New Bintan International Airport;
- (ii) Solar Power Plant and Greenhouse farming;
- (iii) Bintan Motorsport;
- (iv) 2 nd   phase  of  the  Group's  resort  development  (' Kepri Bay ') after the success of the Lagoi Bay Development; and
- (v) New hotels,  commercial/retail  complexes,  and  theme parks.

The  Group  believes  the  above  new  development  will further  increase  the  attractiveness  of  Bintan  Resort  as  a well-connected  eco-tourism  destination  and  spur  the  land demand for tourism-related investment. The Group hopes to conclude some land sales and Joint Ventures in 2023 on the back of improved tourism prospects in Asia.

## BUSINESS REVIEW

## RESORT OPERATION

Tourist  arrival  to  Bintan  Resorts  surged  by  334.6%  from  88,325  in  2021  to  383,819  in  2022  on  the  gradual  and  partial recovery in tourism. Correspondingly, our ferry ridership increased from 123 pax in 2021 to 136,301 pax in 2022. The road to recovery was not an easy feat. During this difficult time, the Group prepared extensively for a seamless and safe travel between Singapore and Bintan, lobbying for a safe travel corridor between both locations and organising major activities/events to bring back tourists. The following major milestones that the Group achieved during 2022:

| Key Date  Activities  Before - 13 Jan 22  Preparation of safe travel to Bintan including:  •  Established audited safe working systems and policies (CHSE in Indonesia and CovSafeSG in  Singapore)  •  Implementation of contact tracing tool  •  Vaccination of all employees and their family members  •  Setting up PCR laboratory for COVID-19 testing  24 Jan 22  Travel Bubble between Bintan Resorts and Singapore.  25 Jan 22  Prime  Minister  Lee  Hsien  Loong  attended  the  Singapore-Indonesia  Leaders'  Retreated  hosted  by  Indonesia President Joko Widodo in The Sanchaya, Bintan Resorts.  25 Feb 22  Two-way quarantine-free travel between Bintan and Singapore started with a group travel agent among  the first international visitors to step foot on Bintan for travel bubble fam trips.  8 Mar 22  Simplification of COVID-19 rules in Bintan Resorts for domestic travelers.  31 Mar 22  Singapore opened international arrival with Vaccinated Travel Lane (VTL) with On Arrival Test (OAT) and  Test before Departure (TBD).  8 Jun 22  Indonesia opened its borders to international visitors.  25 Jun 22  The Embassy of the Republic of Indonesia in Singapore, in collaboration with Bintan Resorts, supports  the rise of tourism in Indonesia, especially in Bintan.  14 - 16 Oct 22  1st mass sports event, Tour de Bintan 2022, was held with more than 500 cyclists participating.  31 Dec 22  KasmaRun and New Year's Eve activities attracted thousands of participants.   |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

Our  Bintan  Resorts,  during  the  World  Tourism  Day  2022 held in Bali on 27 September 2022, received the Sustainable Tourism  Destination  Certificate  and  Certificate  of  Merit Sustainable Tourism Destination for the period 2022 to 2025 from  the  Ministry  of  Tourism  and  Creative  Economy  of  the Republic of Indonesia. The certificates validate the Group's sustainability  programs  to  provide  sustainable  tourism  and reflect  the  Group's  commitment  to  eco-tourism.  The  travel and tourism industry relies on the quality of the surrounding natural environment. Investing in reducing our environmental impact benefits our brand and community and promotes the long-term sustainable development of Bintan Resorts.

There are several new hotels in Bintan Resorts to look forward to in 2023. Each hotel features unique offerings that appeal to a diverse pool of travelers. These new products are expected to cater to a wider demographic and attractions that appeal to all ages.

Hotels joining Bintan Resorts in 2023 are:

- Mövenpick Hotel: 412 rooms and 6 function rooms, the first Mövenpick-managed property in Bintan to offer Swiss-inspired hospitality
- Fourpoint  by  Sheraton: Beachfront,  300+  rooms, 8 Villas, Signature beachfront restaurant
- Hotel  Indigo: 120  rooms  including  5  luxury  villas, 3 culinary beach clubs, resort centre, wedding chapel, and swimming pool
- Holiday Inn Resort: 258  rooms, Ballroom, spa, and swimming pool

In  2022,  the  Group  partnered  with  Resorts  World  Cruises, a  new  Asian  luxury  and  dynamic  cruise  to  make  Bintan Resorts  a  port  of  call.  This  was  not  the  first  time  the Group  collaborated  with  cruise  liners.  The  same  initiative was  established  with  other  cruise  lines  in  2018  but  was disrupted  by  the  COVID-19  pandemic.  The  re-initiation  of this  arrangement shows Bintan Resorts' attractiveness and value  added  to  the  cruise  industry.  At  the  same  time,  the Group is also working with its partners to resume chartered flights to Bintan for Chinese travelers. The Group hopes the restart  of  these  initiatives  will  boost  international  visitors  in the coming years.

The Group is working on the following initiatives to increase the living population in Bintan Island to achieve critical mass for its resort/residential business.

- (i) International  School ;  The  Group  is  working  with one  of  the  largest  owner-operators  of  International Business School to set up operations in Bintan Resorts. The  school  in  Bintan  will  provide  education  services and  short-term  outdoor  education  programs  using experiential learning to foster an individual's values and strengthen their life skills.
- (ii) Independent living facilities ; The Group is discussing with  several  investors  setting  up  facilities  in  Bintan Resort  to  offer  housing,  amenities,  activities,  and services designed for healthy retirement to independent seniors.

With most countries, including China, having completely lifted all travel restrictions, the Group is optimistic that tourist arrival into Bintan will gradually return to pre-pandemic levels.

## OTHER BUSINESS INTERESTS

## AUTOMOTIVE

PT  IMAS  has  reported  net  profit  of  S$53.0  million  in FY2022, reversing from its net loss of S$53.2 million in the corresponding year before. The turnaround came as revenue rose 32.8% to S$2.38 billion as its financial services, trucks and heavy-duty equipment sales, after-sale services, vehicle rental-related income, and mini petrol kiosk business posted double-digit  growth.  Its  associated  companies  has  also contributed significantly to its bottom line with an increased contribution  of  S$22.3  million.  Consequently,  the  Group's share  of  PT  IMAS's  results  was  a  profit  of  S$17.7  million compared to a S$17.6 million loss in FY2021.

<!-- image -->

New developments during the year were as follows:

- Indomobil  Finance  Indonesia  (' IMFI ')  received  the "Indonesia  Most  Acclaimed  Company  2022  with Outstanding Financing Business Development" award in the finance company category, and Indonesia Best CSR Award 2022 with Outstanding Program in Supporting Communities Basic Needs from Warta Ekonomi on 19 May 2022 and 30 June 2022.
- PT IMAS's distributor of the Suzuki brand, PT Suzuki Indomobil Sales, has officially launched the locally-made all-new Suzuki Ertiga Hybrid, its first  electrified  car  in the LMPV class for the domestic market and export to various destination countries around the world.
- PT  IMAS  and  Stellantis  N.V.,  the  parent  company  of Automobiles Citroën, entered a strategic partnership in Oct 2022 to launch the Citroën brand to the Indonesian market in 2023. PT IMAS will be the exclusive distributor of Citroën vehicles and a nonexclusive distributor for the import, promotion, distribution, and sale of spare parts and accessories for 5 years.
- Building on the previous success of its partnership with Seino on the logistic  business,  PT  IMAS  has  entered into another joint venture with Mitsui &amp; Co., Ltd., and Penske  Truck  Leasing  to  establish  a  truck  operating lease business in Indonesia.
- PT  IMAS  signed  MOU  with  ExxonMobil  and  Plastic Energy to assess the potential for large-scale i mplementation of advanced plastic recycling technology in Indonesia to embrace a circular economy in managing plastic waste.
- PT  IMAS  has  entered  into  an  exclusive  partnership with Yadea,  a global leader in developing  and manufacturing  electric  two-wheel  vehicles,  including electric motorcycles, electric mopeds, electric bicycles, and  electric  kid  scooters,  as  the  sole  distributor  for Yadea's high-quality electric vehicles in Indonesia.

## BUSINESS REVIEW

Over  the  years,  PT  IMAS  has  diversified  its  portfolio  of businesses  and  increased  its  market  share  by  expanding its  product  range.  The  Group  is  confident  that  PT  IMAS's integrated and resilient portfolio strategy will enable it to attain long-term profitability  and  sustainable  growth  and  continue contributing positively to the Group.

## OFFSHORE MARINE

The Group's share of profit  from  BOMC was S$1.3 million compared to S$0.5 million in FY2021, an increase of 160.0%. This  was  due  to  the  accelerated  completion  of  its  turnkey projects  in  2022.  With  the  completion  of  2nd  phase  of development,  the  spool  base  facility  for  fabrication  of  the undersea pipeline, BOMC is proceeding with the next phase of  development  for  decommissioning  of  rigs  and  vessels as this  region  is  set  to  become  the  next  key  market  in  the decommissioning of offshore oil and gas assets.

BOMC  has  officially  achieved  its  ISO  9001/14001/45001 accreditation.  Preparation  for  ISO  37001  certification  is  in progress.  This  is  essential  in  developing  BOMC  into  the region's premier offshore marine hub. The Group is confident that BOMC is well-positioned for growth and will contribute significantly.

## OUTLOOK AND FORGING AHEAD

While  the  global  economic  outlook  has  been  clouded  by uncertainties  caused  by  geopolitical  tensions,  the  war  in Ukraine,  rising  prices,  and  higher  interest  costs,  we  will continue to grow our core businesses in Batam and Bintan to the next level and remain focused to:

- (a) expand our industrial parks segment:
- (b) scaling up the deployment of renewable energy in our utility segment;
- (c) drive  tourism  and  related  investment  into  Bintan Resorts;
- (d) optimize the capital structure to manage our cash and liquidity; and
- (e) create a long-term sustainable value for our stakeholders.

## FINANCIAL REVIEW

<!-- image -->

## GROUP FINANCIAL PERFORMANCE

The  Group  revenue  was  S$166.8  million,  15.0%  higher than FY2021's S$145.1 million. It was mainly due to higher revenue from rental and related income and utilities driven by the improved occupancy from the industrial parks segment coupled with improvement in the resort segment as a result of the lifting of travel restrictions in the region and was partially offset  by  lower  contribution  from  the  housing  project  and absence of land sales in the current year.

The Group's cost of sales decreased from S$112.1 million in FY2021 to FY2022's S$111.5 million, mainly due to lower depreciation. The Group's cost of sales to revenue ratio was 0.67  in  FY2022  compared  to  0.77  in  FY2021.  In  line  with the higher revenue, the Group's gross profit increased from S$33.0 million in FY2021 to S$55.3 million in FY2022.

The  Group's  'other  expenses'  was  S$1.1  million  and  were marginally lower than FY2021's S$1.4 million.

The  Group's  'general  and  administrative  expenses'  was S$20.0  million,  higher  than  FY2021's  S$15.6  million,  and was due to low base effect as a result of travel restrictions in 2021 and:

- Higher  manpower-related  costs.  The  increase  in manpower-related  costs  was  mainly  due  to  COVID19-related  cost-saving  measures  such  as  no-pay leave, temporary salary reductions, and suspension of annual bonuses in the previous year. With the gradual reopening of regional economies, the Group reinstated salary and bonus payments in FY2022; and
- Higher professional fees.

The  Group's  'other  operating  expenses'  of  S$24.3  million was comparable to FY2021's S$24.3 million.

The  Group  recorded  a  share  of  profit  from  its  associate companies  of  S$19.1  as  compared  to  FY2021's  loss  of S$16.7 million, mainly due to the following:

- PT  IMAS  contributed  profit  of  S$17.7  million  to  the Group in FY2022 as compared to S$17.6 million loss in FY2021 as:
- (i) PT  IMAS's  revenue  increased  by  S$589.0 million  from  S$1,795.2  million  in  FY2021  to FY2022's S$2,384.2 million, mainly due to higher demand  for  its  financial  services,  higher  trucks and  heavy-duty  equipment  sales,  after-sales services, vehicle rental-related income and higher contributions from its mini petrol kiosk business. In line with higher revenue, PT IMAS's gross profit has increased by S$139.9 million in FY2022; and
- (ii) PT  IMAS's  share  of  its  associate  companies' profit  increased  from  FY2021's S$2.4 million to FY2022's S$24.7 million.
- The Group's share of BOMC's profit was S$1.3 million in FY2022 compared to S$0.5 million in 2021, mainly due to the accelerated completion of turnkey projects.

The Group's 'finance costs' was S$27.3 million, higher than FY2021's S$19.8 million, mainly due to the rising interest rate of external bank borrowings.

The  Group's  net  loss  attributable  to  the  owners  of  the Company  for  FY2022  was  lower  at  S$13.6  million  as compared to FY2021's S$55.6 million.

In  line  with  higher  operating  profit  and  contributions  from associated  companies,  the  Group's  EBITDA  was  S$56.1 million compared to 2021's S$4.9 million.

The Group's basic and diluted net loss per share was 0.25 cents for the year under review.

## SEGMENTAL FINANCIAL PERFORMANCE

## INDUSTRIAL PARKS

Revenue increased from S$38.1 million in FY2021 to S$41.7 million in FY2021 and was mainly due to higher factory rental and related income due to higher occupancy in Batamindo Industrial  Park  and  Bintan  Industrial  Estate  coupled  with the  S$0.3  million  contributions  from  the  housing  project  in Batam. In line with higher revenue, this segment reported a net profit of S$5.8 million in FY2022 compared to $2.2 million in FY2021. EBITDA contribution was S$23.3 million compared to S$22.5 million in FY2021.

## UTILITIES

Revenue increased by S$4.7 million, from S$104.1 million in FY2021 to S$108.8 million in FY2022, mainly due to higher utility consumption in the industrial parks and resorts. Profit decreased  from  S$20.2  million  to  S$7.2  million  and  was mainly due to higher finance costs and a foreign exchange loss  of  S$10.5  million  as  compared  to  a  foreign  exchange gain  of  S$1.5  million  in  FY2021.  EBITDA  contribution  was S$32.2 million compared to S$38.5 million in FY2021.

## PROPERTY DEVELOPMENT

This segment reported a lower net loss of S$10.2 million in FY2022 as compared to S$13.4 million in FY2021 and was mainly due to foreign exchange gain as compared to foreign exchange  loss  in  the  previous  year  but  partially  offset  by higher operating expenses and absence of land sales in the current year as compared to S$1.8 million in FY2021. EBITDA contribution was -S$6.5 million as compared to -S$9.7 million in FY2021.

## RESORT OPERATIONS

Revenue surged by 15 times to S$16.3 million in FY2022 and was mainly  due  to  the  gradual  resumption  of  leisure  travel and tourism activities in Bintan Resorts after the reopening of regional travel. The net loss has reduced from FY2021's S$17.1 million in FY 2021 to FY2022's S$5.4 million. EBITDA contribution was -S$1.2 million compared to -S$12.0 million in FY2021.

## FINANCIAL POSITION

As  at  31  December  2022,  the  Group's  total  assets  were S$1,378.6 million as compared to the previous year-end of S$1,409.8 million.

The Group's property, plant and equipment, and investment properties  decreased  by  S$9.5  million,  mainly  due  to depreciation.

The  Group's  associates  decreased  by  S$30.8  million mainly  due  to  foreign  currency  translation  losses  related to the translation of PT IMAS's results and net assets from Indonesian Rupiah to Singapore dollar, where the Singapore Dollar strengthened as of 31 December 2022.

In  line  with  higher  revenue,  the  Group's  trade  and  other receivables increased by S$1.2 million.

<!-- image -->

## FINANCIAL REVIEW

The  Group's  other  non-current  assets  increased  by  S$3.2 million, mainly due to the recognition of prepaid taxes.

As of  31  December 2022, the Group's total liabilities  were S$619.8  million  compared  to  the  previous  year-end  of S$588.7 million.

The Group's borrowings decreased by S$9.6 million, mainly due to the repayment of the bank borrowings. The Group's total debts, including lease liabilities, as of 31 December 2022 were S$390.2 million as compared to S$389.4 million as of 31 December 2021.

The Group's trade and other payables increased by S$1.2 million,  mainly  due  to  expenses  incurred  from  third  parties for  the  expansion  and  construction  of  new  factories  in  the Group's  industrial  parks  and  joint  development  of  hotels/ resorts in Bintan Resorts.

The  Group's  lease  liabilities  increased  by  S$10.4  million, mainly  due  to  the  recognition  of  the  lease  liability  on  the additional rental of gensets for the utilities business.

The Group's contract liabilities increased by S$35.5 million due to deposits received from the industrial park tenants and higher receipt of deposits from travel agents for purchase of tour packages to Bintan Resorts.

The  Group's  Net  Asset  Value  (' NAV ')  per  share  as  of 31 December 2022 was 13.76 cents.

<!-- image -->

## CASH FLOWS

For the period under review, net cash inflow from operating activities  was  S$22.0  million  compared  to  S$5.0  million generated in the previous period. S$41.2 million of the S$63.2 million cash generated from operating activities was used to finance the payment of income tax and interest.

The Group's net cash used in investing activities of S$14.5 million as compared to S$18.4 million in the previous period, mainly due to increased capital expenditure for constructing new factories in Batamindo Industrial Park.

The Group's net cash used in financing activities of S$13.4 million  was  due  to  higher  borrowing  costs.  The  net  cash received from financing activities in the previous period was mainly due to refinancing syndicated loans.

The Group's cash and cash equivalents were S$127.8 million as of 31 December 2022 compared with S$134.1 million as of 31 December 2021.

## ABOUT THE REPORT

This is the sixth consecutive Sustainability Report (' SR2022 ') published  annually  by  Gallant  Venture  Ltd.  (' GV '  or  the ' Group ').  The  report  provides  an  overview  of  the  Group's priorities,  strategies,  initiatives,  targets,  and  performances with  respect  to  the  Environmental,  Social  and  Governance (' ESG ') issues identified to be material to our businesses and stakeholders.  It  covers  the  financial  year  2022  (' FY2022 ') from 1 January 2022 to 31 December 2022. Stakeholders are advised to refer to this report in conjunction with the Group's Annual Report 2022.

## REPORTING FRAMEWORK

This  report  has  been  prepared  in  accordance  with  the Global Reporting Initiative Sustainability Reporting Standards (' GRI  Standards '), which is the most widely used sustainability  reporting  standard  globally.  We  have  also updated our sustainability disclosures to be aligned with the latest  2021  version  of  its  Universal  Standards.  For  the  full list  of  GRI  references  disclosed  in  this  report,  please  refer to the GRI Content Index on pages 75 to 77, This report is also  aligned  with  the  Sustainability  Reporting  Guide  as  set out  under  the  Singapore  Exchange's  (' SGX ')  Listing  Rules 711A and 711B.

In line with SGX's requirement for mandatory climate-related disclosures, we have started incorporating recommendations by  the  Taskforce  on  Climate-related  Financial  Disclosures (' TCFD ')  in  our  reporting  journey  this  year.  Our  TCFD disclosures can be found on pages 72 to 74. We also actively support the United Nations Sustainable Development Goals (' UN  SDGs ')  and  report  our  contributions  towards  these SDGs.

## REPORTING SCOPE AND BOUNDARIES

GRI 2-2, 2-4

The Group is organised into operating segments according to  the  nature  of  its  products  and  services.  For  a  detailed overview of our operating segments, please refer to our Group structure on page 27. All sustainability data and information presented  in  this  report  relate  to  three  of  the  Group's reportable  segments,  namely  Industrial  Parks,  Utilities  and Resort Operations, unless otherwise stated. We are working towards  the  inclusion  of  data  and  information  from  our Property Development Segment in the next reporting period. There are no restatements of information made in this report.

In  FY2022,  we  re-evaluated  the  Group's  material  ESG topics  through  a  comprehensive  materiality  assessment involving  stakeholder  engagement  and  peer  benchmarking (see page 49). Based on the findings from this year's materiality assessment,  we  continued  to  report  on  the  nine  key  topics

## SUSTAINABILITY REPOR

disclosed in our SR2021 and added three more key topics for this year (see pages 50 to 51). These key topics were identified to  be  the  most  important  and  relevant  to  our  stakeholders. We will  continue  to  review  our  material  topics  on  an  annual basis and ensure that topics relevant to our business and our stakeholders are identified and disclosed adequately.

## REPORTING QUALITY

GRI 2-5

While the Group has not sought external assurance for our report,  we  have  systems  and  internal  controls  in  place  to assess  the  quality  of  data  and  information  reported.  The report  has  undergone  internal  reviews  by  the  Sustainability Reporting  Committee  (' SRC '  or  the  ' Committee ').  Since the  publication  of  our  first  sustainability  report  in  2018,  we have continued to work closely with external consultants to enhance the quality  of  our  report.  In  line  with  the  updated SGX Listing Rules that took effect on 1 January 2022, the sustainability  reporting  process  is  also  being  subjected  to internal  review  by  the  Group's  internal  audit  function  and approved  by  the  Audit  and  Risk  Management  Committee (' ARMC ').  This  internal  review  is  conducted  in  accordance with  the  standards  for  the  Professional  Practice  of  Internal Auditing as prescribed by The Institute of Internal Auditors.

## FEEDBACK

GRI 2-3

The Group values the perspectives of all our stakeholders and strives  to  continuously  improve  our  sustainability  practices and performances. We welcome any feedback, suggestions, questions, or comments on our report, as well as on how we can drive our sustainability commitments. Please share them with us at: gallant@gallantventure.com.

## OUR OPERATIONS

GRI 2-1, 2-6

## Core Business Operations

The Group operates in four core business segments, namely Utilities,  Industrial  Parks,  Resort  Operations  and  Property Development. Through the Group's associated companies' PT Indomobil Sukses International Tbk (' PT IMAS ') and BOMC Pte Ltd (' BOMC '), the Group has foothold in the automotive and offshore marine industry. Our Group Structure, showing a complete list of companies can be found on page 27 of the FY2022 Annual Report.

<!-- image -->

## Operating Countries

GV  is  headquartered  and  listed  in  Singapore,  with  core operations  in  Batam  and  Bintan  Islands,  Indonesia.  Our businesses  leverage  the  strategic  proximity  of  Singapore and close economic cooperation between the Singapore and Indonesian  governments  to  promote  investments  in  Batam and Bintan.

## SUPPLY CHAIN

Our talent pool consists of skilled professionals catering to diverse business operations. We also work closely with other personnel including consultants, contractors, and equipment suppliers  to  outsource  engineering  and  construction  work concerning our business development activities. Our supply chain  consists  of  279  suppliers  mainly  from  Singapore, Indonesia, Malaysia, and the United Kingdom. Approximately 88%  of  suppliers  are  based  in  Indonesia,  where  our  core operations are located.

We strive to source products in a responsible manner and establish  strong  working  relationships  with  our  suppliers. As  part  of  our  sustainability  strategy,  we  integrate  ESG considerations into the procurement processes and perform regular  assessments  of  key  suppliers  to  ensure  that  they meet our requirements for quality, sustainable practices and compliance  with  relevant  laws  and  legislations.  We  believe that close collaboration with them can increase the resilience of our supply chain to shocks and help position our business for growth.

This  provided  a  better  understanding  of our exposure to climate-related risks and opportunities, allowing us to make better informed business decisions.

## CORPORATE GOVERNANCE

GRI 2-12, 2-13, 2-14, 2-16, 2-17, 2-26

## Sustainability Management

The  Group  has  established  a  governance  structure  for  sustainability-related  issues  involving  the  Board,  the  Executive Committee as well as the management team. In line with the new SGX Listing Rules effective 1 January 2022, the Group has also arranged for all directors to undergo relevant sustainability training to ensure that they keep abreast with the ever-evolving ESG landscape and better manage sustainability-related issues. The Group will continue to ensure that all directors receive adequate training and are equipped with the necessary knowledge about sustainability.

Figure 1: GV's Sustainability Governance Structure

<!-- image -->

| Board of  Directors  SR  Committee  Management  The Board maintains overall responsibility and oversight over environmental,  social and economic-related matters. Integration of sustainability within the  highest level of governance ensures the long-term resilience of our operations  through future-proofing against pertinent risks.  The Committee is chaired by the Executive Director/Chief  Financial  Officer  ('CFO') and comprises senior management representatives from respective  business  departments.  The  Committee  oversees  the  Group's  sustainability  performance  and  initiatives  through  the  evaluation  of  material  ESG  topics,  and also provides progress updates and recommendations to the Board on  the Group's overall sustainability performance.  The Management works closely with the Committee to identify, manage and  mitigate material ESG risks and opportunities.   |
|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

The Group has also established a separate risk governance structure, led by the Audit and Risk Management Committee (' ARMC ') and Management, and with oversight by the Board. Our risk management policies and framework have also been developed with guidance and alignment with the United Nations Precautionary Principle Approach (Article 15). Based on our risk management framework, reviews by the ARMC, Management and internal auditors are conducted regularly to ensure that the Group's internal controls are adequate and effective in addressing the financial, operational, compliance and information technology risks.

The Group conducts our business in accordance with the MAS Code of Corporate Governance 2018 (' Code ') administered by SGX, which is aligned with global corporate governance best practices. We apply the 13 Principles of the Code throughout our business operations and maintain accountability, transparency, sustainability and ethicality through business practices. This helps to ensure that the best interests of all stakeholders are served while achieving continued business performance. For more details on our corporate governance policies, please refer to pages 79 to 101.

## MATERIALITY ASSESSMENT

GRI 3-1, 3-2

## Methodology

Our Group discloses material ESG topics deemed relevant to our business and stakeholders in the annual sustainability report. We also review these material topics every year to ensure that relevant topics are identified and adequately disclosed, in line with the GRI Reporting Principles.

In FY2022, the SRC conducted a comprehensive materiality assessment in a step-by-step process with the assistance of an external consultant and involvement of the Group's senior management:

| Materiality Assessment Process  Key Activities  Identification of relevant topics  •  Review of relevant sustainability topics disclosed by industry peers  •  Stakeholder  engagement  with  both  internal  and  external  stakeholders  to  accurately identify and address their areas of concern  Prioritisation of identified topics  •  Ranking  of  identified  topics  through  a  stakeholder  survey,  in  which  the  importance of each topic is rated on a scale based on the significance of its  economic, environmental and social impact  Assessment of prioritised topics  •  Gap analysis of prioritised topics against the material topics disclosed in our last  sustainability report (SR2021) to highlight key topics to be disclosed in this and  subsequent reporting cycles  Validation of selected material topics  •  Validation of the materiality matrix, selected material topics and disclosures for  this reporting cycle with the SRC and Board   |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

We surveyed a total of 28 stakeholders - 20 internal stakeholders and 8 external stakeholders - to understand their perspective on the Group's material topics.

The industry peer research and stakeholder survey results were incorporated in a gap analysis of prioritised topics against the material topics disclosed in SR2021, allowing us to assess and select key topics that matter most to our business and stakeholders.  The  SRC  reviewed  and  validated  the  list  of  selected  material  topics  for  FY2022  before  submitting  it  to  the Board for approval. The materiality assessment process above has allowed us to identify key areas of concern material to our business and external stakeholders.

## BUSINESS FOUNDATION

## ECONOMIC SUSTAINABILITY

GRI 2-23, 2-24, 2-25, 3-3, 201

Economic sustainability refers to the ability of an economic system to maintain its productivity and viability over the long term while minimising negative impacts on the environment and society.

Our Group's long-term success relies heavily on the economic performance, which is why we are dedicated to generating economic value and ensuring sustained earnings for our shareholders. Our commitment to economic sustainability requires a balance between economic growth, social equity, and environmental protection. Through the efficient use of resources, minimisation of waste and investment in sustainable technologies and infrastructure, GV seeks to reduce the environmental footprint of economic activities while also improving economic efficiency and competitiveness.

The  Group  also  seeks  to  create  economic  value  while  also  ensuring  the  long-term  sustainability  of  our  operations.  This involves adopting sustainable business practices, investing in sustainable technologies, and engaging in responsible resource management. By doing so, we consider the long-term impacts of our economic activities on people, communities and the planet, rather than just focusing on short-term gains.

## ANTI-CORRUPTION

GRI 2-23, 2-24, 2-25, 2-26, 2-27, 3-3, 205

The Group is dedicated to conducting itself with honesty and adhering to ethical practices at all times. We have implemented strong  measures  to  prevent  corruption  and  protect  our business continuity. We have a zero-tolerance policy against all  forms  of  unethical  corporate  behavior,  including  bribery, corruption and fraud. All employees, including Directors and Officers,  are  bound  by  our  Anti-Corruption  Policy,  which includes anti-corruption principles and guidance.

While the Group has developed a comprehensive Anti-Corruption Policy,  we  recognise  that  the  Anti-Corruption  Policy  cannot anticipate  every  possible  scenario  that  an  employee  may encounter  while  working.  As  a  result,  we  strongly  advise employees to promptly seek guidance from their supervisors, the Human Resource (' HR ') Department, the Legal Department, or senior management if they are uncertain about a situation.

The Anti-Corruption Policy has three main objectives:

1. To establish  the  responsibilities  of  the  Group  and  each employee  in  maintaining  the  Group's  zero-tolerance approach towards corruption and bribery.
2. To  provide  employees  with  information  and  guidance on how to identify, address, resolve, avoid, and prevent instances of corruption and bribery that may arise during their employment.
3. To offer clear guidance and explanations regarding ethical conduct, compliance with laws, anti-corruption measures, acceptance  of  gifts  and  hospitality,  and  donations  and contributions.

Our  Anti-Corruption  Policy  undergoes  regular  reviews  and revisions  to  align  with  the  Group's  evolving  procedures. We evaluate its effectiveness by assessing the outcomes of various preventive, detective and responsive anti-corruption measures.

| Types of measures  Key measures in place  Preventive measures  •  Code of business conduct  •  Employee code of conduct  •  Conflict of interest policy  •  Corporate gift policy  •  Fraud risk assessments  •  Third-party due diligence  Detective measures  •  Whistle-blowing policy  •  Forensic data analysis  •  Compliance and monitoring  •  Pre-employment screening  Responsive measures  •  Fraud reporting procedures  •  Fraud investigation procedures  •  Grievance handling procedures   |
|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

## SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

All  new  hires  must  also  comply  with  our  policies  from  the start  of  their  employment.  Furthermore,  the  Group  has implemented rigorous controls over its procurement processes to emphasise our commitment to ethical business practices, which signals our suppliers about our zero-tolerance policy towards corruption.

We aim to maintain communicating our anti-corruption policies and procedures to 100% of the Board and employees. We also  aim  to  maintain  a  100%  coverage  of  the  Board  and employees that have received training on anti-corruption.

## Performance

We  are  pleased  to  announce  that  since  the  start  of  our sustainability reporting journey in 2017, there have been no significant violations of our anti-corruption policies identified.

In  FY2022,  the  Group's  Anti-Corruption  Policy  and  other relevant  policies  and  procedures  were  communicated  to 100%  of  the  Board  and  employees (3) .  We  also  achieved a  100%  coverage  of  the  Board  and  employees  that  have received training on anti-corruption.

For more details on our anti-corruption performance, please refer  to  the  ESG  Performance  Data  section  under  the Appendix.

## ENVIRONMENT

## ECO-EFFICIENT OPERATIONS

GRI 2-23, 2-24, 2-25, 3-3, 302, 303, 305

The  Group  is  cognisant  that  our  operations  contribute  to a  wide  range  of  impacts,  and  therefore,  is  dedicated  to taking  climate  action,  enhancing  resource  efficiency,  and decreasing  our  environmental  footprint.  To  achieve  this goal,  we  have  established  various  measures  to  increase operational  efficiency  and  minimise  the  use  of  energy, water  and  generation  of  waste.  Our  compliance  controls are  designed  to  monitor  our  operations  and  ensure  that we  comply  with  relevant  environmental  regulations  while improving  our  operational  efficiency.  We  also  engage  our key stakeholders through different channels to address their concerns and incorporate their feedback into our business approach and strategy.

## GV's eco-efficient operations strategy involves several initiatives, including:

1. Energy Efficiency: GV has been working to improve energy efficiency  in  its  properties  by  adopting  energy-efficient technologies and practices such as LED lighting, motion sensors, and efficient air conditioning systems.
2.  Waste Management:  GV  has implemented waste management  strategies  such  as  reducing  the  use  of single-use  plastics,  recycling  waste,  and  composting organic waste.

(3) Employees are provided at a Group-level, including the other business segment - Property Development.

## SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

3. Water  Conservation:  GV  has  been  promoting  water conservation by using water-efficient fixtures and fittings and implementing water recycling and harvesting systems.
4. Sustainable  Transportation:  GV  has  been  encouraging sustainable  transportation  options  such  as  electric vehicles,  bicycles,  and  public  transportation  for  its employees and customers.
5. Green Building: GV has been constructing and retrofitting buildings  with  eco-friendly  materials  and  technologies, such as green roofs and solar panels.

## Energy and Emissions

Climate change will inevitably have a significant impact on our business operations, and the Group recognises that we need to take urgent action to limit the effects of climate change. In  2022,  we  started  to  align  with  the  TCFD  framework, covering  key  physical  and  transition  risks  that  may  impact our businesses. For more details on our TCFD disclosures, please refer to pages 72 to 74.

As  an  energy  provider,  we  recognise  that  effective  energy and  emissions  management  is  critical  to  minimising  our environmental footprint, as well as reducing our operational costs. We regularly monitor and evaluate how our business operations contribute to climate change to identify areas for improvement that inform our sustainability priorities. In 2022, we have also taken the step to include Scope 2 emissions in our GHG emissions inventory. Furthermore, we are looking to expand our GHG inventory to include Scope 3 categories in the next reporting period.

## Our energy and emissions management strategy aims to:

- Create a balanced business portfolio that considers both the  financial  expectations  of  our  stakeholders  and  our businesses' impact on the climate.
- Fulfill  our duty to protect the environment and conserve resources  while  providing  competitive  and  dependable solutions for our stakeholders.
- Enhance energy efficiency by adopting best practices and economically viable solutions.

As we strive for profitability in our utility business, we also aim to balance our financial goals with our commitment to reducing our environmental footprint. We have developed optimisation strategies to identify opportunities for cost and internal usage reductions while staying mindful of our environmental impact.

We have taken several new initiatives and measures to reduce our energy consumption and carbon footprint, such as:

- We  have  partnered  with  a  green  energy  product  and solution provider to installed solar photovoltaic system to supply up to 20% of green energy to our tenants in the industrial parks in Batam by 2023.
- We will also roll up the same initiative to supply the green energy to our industrial park and resorts in Bintan.
- We have allocated up to 1,000 ha of land in Bintan for further development  of  solar  farms  to  increase  supply  of  green energy to our tenants and customers in the next phase.
- Exploring  emerging  low-carbon  technologies  such  as green  hydrogen  and  carbon  capture  that  will  help  to reduce our carbon footprint in the longer term.

Our  goals  are  to  achieve  80%  efficiency  in  our  power generation process every year and reduce our GHG emissions by  10%  from  our  2017  baseline  year  by  2024.  In  2022, we  obtained  93%  efficiency  rate  and  achieved  a  15.4% reduction  in  GHG  emissions  from  2017  (17,962.0  tCO 2 e). If  there  are  any  significant  deviations  from  these  goals,  we will flag and escalate to senior management in the form of a monthly report for prompt advice on corrective actions and remediation measures.

## Water

Similar to energy and emissions, the Group is committed to building a future of sustainable water usage and minimising the negative effects associated with excessive water consumption. With  our  business  segments  being  heavily  reliant  on  water supply,  we  recognise  the  importance  of  preserving  this essential resource. We aim to minimise our impact on water resources  by  implementing  best  practices  in  our  operations and supply chain. This includes reducing water usage through efficient  water  management  practices,  promoting  water conservation  initiatives,  and  ensuring  that  the  water  we  use is sourced responsibly and sustainably. We also work closely with our suppliers and stakeholders to ensure they share our commitment to sustainable water sourcing and conservation. Our ultimate goal is to operate in a way that safeguards water resources and ensures their availability for future generations.

Our water management strategy strives to create a balanced business  portfolio  that  considers  our  stakeholders'  financial expectations and our businesses' impact on the climate. We also perform environmental impact assessments in accordance with national and international standards and practices (such as the WHO Standard Guidelines and ISO 14001) to identify environmental risks and support water conservation. Moreover, we have taken various steps and launched several initiatives to diminish our water usage by enhancing the effectiveness of our water treatment facilities. This includes:

- Benchmarking with key industry practices to set, promote, and achieve performance targets.

(8) Our methodology for computing the water consumption is based on the difference between the water withdrawn and water sold for the reporting period.

- Evaluating new technology for producing water; and
- Monitoring,  evaluating,  and  analysing  infrastructure performance  and  identifying  any  adjustments  that  are needed to further improve efficiencies.

Our  goal  is  to  attain  an  80%  efficiency  rate  in  our  water treatment process every year. In 2022, we achieved this goal and obtained an 87% efficiency rate. Similar to energy and emissions, we will flag any significant deviations from these goals and escalate them to senior management.

## Supplier Environmental Assessment

We  believe  that  building  a  secure  and  trustworthy  supply chain  is  crucial  to  ensuring  the  smooth  operation  of  our production activities; hence, we added supplier environmental assessment to our list of material topics for this year. We also actively advocate for local suppliers whenever possible and work with them to identify improvement areas and implement sustainable practices.

As  part  of  our  commitment  to  sustainability,  we  conduct supplier environmental assessments to ensure that our  suppliers  meet  our  environmental  standards.  These assessments include evaluations of suppliers' environmental management  systems,  their  compliance  with  relevant environmental regulations and laws, and their environmental performance.  A  new  supplier  can  only  be  added  to  our Approved Supplier List if all requirements are fulfilled. Through these  assessments,  we  aim  to  reduce  the  environmental impact of our supply chain and promote sustainable business practices throughout our operations.

While  we  currently  do  not  have  a  target  for  our  supplier environmental performance, we are working towards setting one in the next reporting period.

GRI 2-7, 2-8, 2-23, 2-24, 2-25, 3-3, 401, 404, 405

GV recognises that  building  a  talented  and  motivated  workforce  is  vital  to  our  continued  success.  We  are  committed  to attracting,  developing  and  retaining  employees  who  are  fully  engaged  in  advancing  our  mission,  while  also  cultivating  a conducive organisational culture and workplace that prioritises diversity, equality, safety and meritocracy. To achieve this, we uphold fair employment practices, provide ample learning and development opportunities, and strive to create an environment where all employees feel valued and supported.

## Employment

Recognising that employees are essential to the growth of our businesses, the Group places significant emphasis on employee satisfaction and has implemented policies to design remuneration and benefits that effectively attract, retain, and motivate top talent. To achieve this, we ensure that our remuneration packages are appropriately set, in line with the Group's long-term interests and risk policies. By doing so, we aim to create a workplace culture that attracts and retains exceptional employees who share our values and vision for the future.

In addition to these initiatives, we have also fostered an open and transparent culture where employees are encouraged to share their feedback, suggestions, and grievances with the HR or Legal Department. The Group strictly prohibits any form of retaliation against employees for complaints made in good faith. In the event that such retaliation is detected, the case will be reported directly to the CEO. By cultivating a culture of openness and trust, we aim to foster greater employee engagement and build a more resilient organisation that can withstand challenges and adapt to changes in the business environment.
